12. Radiation/optical sources, heating, vacuum operations and instrument access are simulated educational systems with independent functional safety logic; no real-world procedural setpoints are implied.
13. W179 disconnection must change actual topology and invalidate trusted analytical pressure without changing true chamber pressure to zero.
14. On W179 loss, inhibit protected heating/mass analysis, ramp modeled hazardous energy down and preserve the pristine core in its separate enclosure.
15. Keep Objectives 1–5 historically certified after deliberate W179 fault; only Objective 6 needs a **new complete qualified** run.
16. Three instrument failures must change actual state and evidence; merely setting an alarm flag cannot satisfy fault recovery.
17. Prevent conflicting scene rebuilds from destroying player wires, saved science evidence, terminal hosts or previous levels.
18. Explicitly report missing models/materials, graph conflicts, rejected commands and uncertified evidence. Never claim 6/6 unless actual `LevelGamePlayManager` verifies it.

# 32. FAILURE INJECTION SYSTEM — THREE REAL STATE MUTATIONS

Implement exactly three authoritatively monitored disturbances: (A) inert-glovebox purge degradation; (B) CT stage encoder slip; (C) mass detector gain drift. # 33. FAULT A — PURGE DEGRADATION AND CONTAMINATION

Faulty inert purge valve or circulation state increases contamination witness under a reproducible fictional environmental model. Sample transfers must be inhibited before the pristine archive is compromised when measurements are trustworthy. The player diagnoses valve-command/actual mismatch, restores the affected component, purges/requalifies only the **handling environment**, and demonstrates new acceptable contamination witness samples. If actual sample exposure already occurred, preserve evidence of alteration; do not relabel contaminated material as unexposed. A successful repair requires normal behavior over a qualification interval.

# 34. FAULT B — CT STAGE ENCODER SLIP

Inject systematic offset, missed counts or intermittent slip between the stage's actual angular position and the reported encoder. Scans then reconstruct incorrectly even though the motor is moving. The player compares reference fiducial and independent geometric checks, inhibits new certification, repairs/calibrates encoder response, discards/invalidate affected projections as appropriate and acquires new complete coverage. The certification evaluator must explicitly detect a reconstruction built from corrupted stage-angle evidence.

# 35. FAULT C — MASS DETECTOR GAIN DRIFT

Change mass detector signal gain/offset with a slow modeled drift that distorts peak intensities and compositional ratios. A spectrum may still appear visually clear but be invalid. The player checks a safe fictional reference measurement, diagnoses detector response mismatch, repairs/calibrates the gain, and re-acquires a qualified available sample or reserved test standard. Old spectra remain historical faulty evidence; no retroactive correction without explicit recalibration processing and provenance.

# 36. W179 VERIFIED ENDPOINTS AND SINGLE CRITICAL ID

**Critical source-derived route:** `W179: L93_AnalyticalVacuumPressureTransducer / P180 / TRUSTED_CHAMBER_PRESSURE_OUT → L93_AnalyticalSafetyPLC / P244 / TRUSTED_CHAMBER_PRESSURE_IN`. This is the sole critical route in the Level 93 conceptual wiring manifest. `P180` and `P244` are decorative anchor IDs, not automatic live terminals. Build independent type-compatible Project Spark terminals and actual player wire continuity for this route; preserve exact visible anchor mapping. No hidden backup edge, mirror telemetry signal or SCADA graph should satisfy the critical-authority input.

# 37. OBJECTIVE 6 — W179 DISCONNECT / SAFE SHUTDOWN

After authoritative historical Objectives 1–5 have passed, begin a supervised low-energy fault-test qualification. Have the player physically remove **W179** with the actual game's wire interaction tool. Verify electrical graph edge removal and invalidate trusted chamber pressure. Reject new heater/mass source power, transition plant through bounded protected shutdown (no instantaneous true pressure/temperature changes), latch safety fault, preserve specimen custody and keep independent protections effective. An on-screen dial that continues to show a last known value is labeled *stale* and may never count as safety evidence. Tests must prove W179 cannot be bypassed by old values, visually reattached fake cable, other unrelated telemetry, or a forced scene objective update.

# 38. W179 REPAIR, CALIBRATE, RESET AND FRESH END-TO-END SCIENCE

The player reconnects W179 between correct **live** functional terminals, not merely touching nearby art. Confirm graph continuity, proper control power, pressure sensor measurement quality, timestamps, calibration version and independent chamber condition. Require an explicit operator safety-reset event after all preconditions; reconnection alone is insufficient. Create a **new qualification run ID** and perform fresh CT projections, fresh Raman/IR/XRF science evidence and fresh micro-sample mass analysis using finite reserved or currently available material; rebuild the final cross-instrument report. Preserve previous Objectives 1–5 historically, but do not reuse their evidence to satisfy Objective 6. Award Objective 6 only from this new complete run and safety-certified results.

# 39. SIX EXACT OBJECTIVES FROM SOURCE CSV

Keep the exact source objective titles, IDs and high-level acceptance definitions:

1. **Build the Elemental Analysis Network:** 194 real user wires; valid SparkTerminal graph, supply returns and independent protection.
2. **Commission the Pristine Science Laboratory:** Custody, inert handling, calibrated CT/optical/XRF/vacuum/mass instruments with fresh evidence.
3. **Reveal the Hidden Mineral Archive:** Real CT projection coverage, Raman/infrared/XRF spectra, uncertainty-aware cross-instrument material map.
4. **Decode the Ancient Chemical Signature:** Finite allocated mass, protected thermal release, vacuum-qualified mass spectra, remainder preserved.
5. **Recover Three Scientific Instrument Failures:** Actual purge degradation, CT encoder slip, detector gain drift, repair and fresh requalification.
6. **Restore Critical Analytical Chamber Feedback:** Physical W179 disconnect, safe energy inhibit, reconnection, fresh calibration, explicit reset and new full scientific qualification.

Source `Implemented=NO` for all six. Build real evaluators with typed evidence and historical completion tracking. Do not award objectives through animations, `OnButtonClicked`, UI labels or completion commands.

# 40. OBJECTIVE 1 — LIVE ELECTRICAL CERTIFICATION

Gate on all 194 required edges being present and correctly typed in the solver with valid graph connectivity, control power/returns, no incompatible shorts, correct safety chain and unique wire IDs. Special-case W179 as required but do not make it permanently unremovable: Objective 1 is a historical commissioning certificate that can remain complete when deliberately disconnected during Objective 6. During any active protected operation, safety controls must still react to topology changes. Test wrong endpoint, missing return, accidental multi-pin short, duplicated wire, detached cable and restored connection.

# 41. OBJECTIVE 2 — LABORATORY COMMISSIONING EVIDENCE

Require sample custody identity, qualified inert environment, trusted contamination witness, functioning transfer path, CT stage calibration/geometry, Raman/IR/XRF reference checks, protected vacuum pressure calibration, mass detector reference gain and independent safety self-test. All evidence is fresh and tied to correct device serial/configuration/run. An instrument powered indicator alone is not calibration.

# 42. OBJECTIVE 3 — NON-DESTRUCTIVE GEOLOGY EVIDENCE

Require multiple actual measured CT angular projections with adequate coverage, valid reconstruction quality, region-registered Raman/IR/XRF scans, calibration/uncertainty and a cross-instrument interpretation. Gate on agreement or explicitly supported competing hypotheses rather than an unconditional unique mineral claim. Main core mass must remain unchanged during non-destructive acquisition. Failed stage encoder data must invalidate the affected analysis.

# 43. OBJECTIVE 4 — FINITE MICRO-SAMPLE & CHEMICAL EVIDENCE

Require a physically tracked micro-sample allocation and custody, valid protected transfer into a separate chamber, qualified pressure/safety conditions, thermal release with finite energy/mass, measured mass spectrum and coherent sample preservation record. The remaining archive must have a valid seal and correct mass balance. Trigger neither Objective 4 nor restored sample quantity merely from `MassSpectrumComplete = true`.

# 44. OBJECTIVE 5 — THREE FAULT RECOVERIES WITH FRESH RUN

Each of the three specified failures must be injected, detected through physical/measurement symptoms, repaired through player action and demonstrated recovered by a fresh relevant test. After **all three**, require a new end-to-end science analysis run with new CT/spectral/analytical evidence. Preserve previous measurements for audit but invalidate them as new-qualification evidence when faulted. Ensure no player can skip a disturbance by avoiding the instrument or restarting the scene.

# 45. OBJECTIVE 6 — PROVABLE W179 RECOVERY

Evaluate a chronologically ordered sequence: historical 1–5 → supervised test authorized → actual W179 disconnection → real missing trusted feedback → bounded shutdown and latch → actual W179 reconnection → fresh pressure calibration + independent safe conditions → explicit safety reset → new qualification run → new CT, optical/elemental and mass-analysis evidence → correct finite material ledger and preserved archive → final safety pass. Only then notify the real `LevelGamePlayManager` through its verified API that Objective 6 passed. All stages must be testable with recorded timestamps, event IDs and evidence hashes.

# 46. SCADA: LIVE INSTRUMENTS AND SCIENTIFIC DIGITAL TWIN

Build panels for specimen ID/custody, archive mass, reserved/consumed material, contamination witness, inert transfer state, CT stage command vs encoder, projection coverage, reconstruction confidence, Raman/IR/XRF measurements, protected chamber **true simulated state vs reported sensor**, heater/vacuum pump actual states, detector gain, spectra, qualified report and W179 continuity/freshness. Show the six exact objectives and fault step diagnostics.

Visual stage screens can depict voxel slices, spectral curves, elemental maps and mass peaks, but every curve is generated from authoritative data, including noise, misalignment and failure. Add high-contrast color-blind-safe status cues and labels. Historical results marked archived/invalid cannot look indistinguishable from current trusted samples. False success by changing a UI color must be impossible.

# 47. PLAYER INTERACTIONS, ACCESSIBILITY AND LEARNING LOOP

Use existing Project Spark interaction, shopping/inventory and wiring tools. Interactables include instrument control switches, sensor calibration actions, safe sample transfer commands, finite micro-sample selection, CT scan acquisition, spectral sampling and scientific report review. Avoid giant multi-input debug menus that bypass process controls. Display contextual cause/effect: e.g. “CT geometry calibration missing,” “Chamber pressure signal stale after W179 disconnect,” “Insufficient reserved fragment,” “Mass spectrum contains unqualified detector gain.” Provide optional guided tasks and advanced free-operation mode with the same authoritative simulation, not separate success shortcuts.

# 48. SUGGESTED RUNTIME COMPONENT RESPONSIBILITIES

**Proposed new classes only; do not claim they exist:** `SparkLevel93Coordinator` (mission state), `SparkLevel93SampleArchive` (custody/ledger), `SparkLevel93MaterialTruth` (deterministic geology), `SparkLevel93InertHandlingPlant` (transfer and contamination), `SparkLevel93TomographySystem` (stage/forward measurements/reconstruction), `SparkLevel93SpectroscopySystem` (Raman/IR/XRF), `SparkLevel93VacuumPlant` (true chamber physics), `SparkLevel93ThermalAnalyzer` (finite thermal release), `SparkLevel93MassSpectrometer` (finite gas/mass measurements), `SparkLevel93ScienceEvidence` (measurement provenance/uncertainty), `SparkLevel93AnalyticalSafetyPLC` (independent safety), `SparkLevel93FaultDirector` (three real faults), `SparkLevel93W179Recovery` (critical test), `SparkLevel93ObjectiveTracker` (6/6 evidence) and `SparkLevel93VisualRigAdapter` (read-only rendering).

Design interfaces after inspecting existing Project Spark assemblies and component patterns. Use ScriptableObject data for scenario thresholds and serializable state for save/reload. Keep business/project global code changes minimal.

# 49. ONE-CLICK GAMEPLAY BUILDER — IDEMPOTENT AND SAFE

Add `Tools/Project Spark/Levels/Create Level 93` as a **new Editor** menu; it is distinct from Level93 art menus 1–4. Builder actions: validate source manifest/geometry, create or upgrade Level 93 definition and prefab under the existing level asset convention, create stable scene roots, place art only if missing or on explicit visual rebuild, create independent runtime component instances, map the 272 visible P anchors, generate the 388 functional contact instances correctly, define the 194 wiring objectives without connecting them, bind instrument/safety states, spawn SCADA, configure six source-authored objective definitions and add source validation reports.

Provide dry-run and rebuild modes. Rerunning must preserve user-placeable wires, persistent sample/material ledger, saved evidence and previous levels. Use `AssetDatabase.StartAssetEditing` safely with `try/finally`, Undo where appropriate, validation before mutating assets, deterministic stable component IDs and no modification of Levels 1–92. Finish with a truthful build report listing changed files, warnings and missing references. A separate option may deliberately reset the Level 93 **test-only** save state with explicit confirmation.

# 50. LEVEL PREVIEW TABLE / SEPARATE DISPLAY MOUNT

Make the generated Level 93 art compatible with Project Spark's futuristic game-room central preview table using visual-only mesh copies, normalized bounds and a preview host. The preview can show the sample archive, CT and spectroscopy arrangement on a clean platform. Strip or disable real gameplay scripts, level evaluators, terminal components, rigidbody physics and player interactions in preview instances. No duplicate player purchases or duplicated electrical graph from a miniature table scene. The playable source level remains unaffected.

# 51. EDUCATIONAL GAMEPLAY TUNING — CONFIGURABLE FICTIONAL VALUES

Provide named tunables for sample initial mass and reservations, CT number of angular views and minimum geometric coverage, stage speed/settling, spectral acquisition noise and duration, inert contamination witness qualification interval, vacuum response time and pressure-trust freshness, detector drift rate, simulation time scale, power/temperature boundaries, sample seal test and certification uncertainty. Establish coherent starter defaults in ScriptableObject assets but identify them as *game tuning*, not physical instrument recipes. Use deterministic presets for normal run, three faults and W179 test. Avoid hard-coded “press button 60 seconds” objectives; require real evidence within configured tolerances.

# 52. DIAGNOSTIC CATEGORIES — SPECIFIC PLAYER FEEDBACK

Provide at least: missing wire/incorrect port type; control return missing; shared visual anchor with incompatible signals; sensor not powered; stale critical sample; invalid calibration; sample custody mismatch; lost inert qualification; stage actual angle out of tolerance; insufficient CT views; Raman/IR spectral confidence low; XRF emission background invalid; no available micro-sample; chamber not isolated; pressure not trusted; protected heater start rejected; mass gain drift; failed seal; consumed material cannot be restored; W179 disconnected; safety reset denied; new post-repair run incomplete. Show device name, requested action and reason; do not reveal hidden geological truth as a cheat.

# 53. UNIT, EDITOR, PLAY MODE, INTEGRATION AND REGRESSION TEST PLAN

Test scene import in a clean Unity project with the actual render pipeline, then in the production Project Spark project. Record actual shader/compiler warnings and generated `.mat` status. Automation should cover graph connectivity, mixed-signal contacts, finite mass/conservation, cross-instrument truth consistency, CT encoder geometry, spectral uncertainty, vacuum sensor trust, physical process transitions, three repairs, W179 exact sequence, persistence and all six objectives. Test each negative path with stale/fake data and malicious UI attempts. Add performance profiling on target hardware and Levels 1–92 regression.

**Acceptance tests are requirements, not already-passed results.** Archive test-run evidence and a machine-readable report containing expected/actual, runtime version, sample seed and pass/fail. A structural FBX/GLB validation record alone is not gameplay testing.

# 54. MANDATORY DELIVERABLES FROM IMPLEMENTER

Deliver complete commented C# source and Editor builder; compiled project assemblies with zero new errors; Level 93 ScriptableObject definition and scene/prefab; functioning 194-wire puzzle, logical terminals and interaction hosts; full sample/CT/spectroscopy/mass analysis plant; contamination and safety PLC; W179 recovery; SCADA and read-only visuals; player tutorial and input feedback; test presets; updated model/material/collider bindings; six evaluator implementations; reproducible acceptance test suite; documented editor steps; and an honest validation report with a full 0/6→6/6 Play Mode walkthrough. Preserve Levels 1–92, avoid project-wide changes without a written rationale, and do not replace FBX assets with images or fake 2D art.

# 55. COMPLETE PLAYER RUN — FROM 0/6 TO 6/6

1. Enter the Elemental Archive laboratory; inspect sample identification and source instrument network.
2. Install and verify all 194 real electrical connections with correct returns and protection.
3. Commission inert vault/sample custody and sensor calibration.
4. Calibrate CT stage, source/detector, Raman, IR, XRF, vacuum pressure and mass gain.
5. Collect valid CT projections from actual stage motion and reconstruct geological layers.
6. Acquire region-mapped Raman/IR/XRF spectra and reconcile material candidates.
7. Allocate a finite micro-sample, transfer it through a qualified path and verify vacuum readiness.
8. Execute protected modeled thermal release and record actual mass spectra with provenance.
9. Produce a qualified cross-instrument geological report and return/protect remainder.
10. Inject and recover purge degradation, CT encoder slip and detector gain drift.
11. Requalify the full science workflow using fresh measurements and remaining material.
12. Enter the supervised W179 test, disconnect physically, observe true electrical loss and protected shutdown.
13. Reconnect W179, recalibrate pressure, confirm safe physical conditions and explicitly reset PLC.
14. Begin a **new run ID**, reacquire CT + Raman/IR/XRF + finite mass analysis, preserve sample balance.
15. Verify actual `LevelGamePlayManager` certificates reach **6/6** and trigger mission cinematic from real completion state.

# 56. FINAL QUALITY BAR — THE ELEMENTAL ARCHIVE

The finished Level 93 should feel like a professionally engineered spacecraft science mission. A pristine KHEPRI-7 core sits in a gold-trimmed vault. Players construct a real, interactive control network, observe instruments taking imperfect measurements, compare reconstructed layers and spectra, protect finite samples, repair three true measurement/process faults, and demonstrate trustworthy independent safety feedback. The final report must emerge from consistent data, not arbitrary toggles. Objective completion is earned by actual physics/electrical/scientific evidence, fully tested in the existing Unity project. Cinematic ending: holographic core expands, verified mineral-region overlays and analytical curves align, protected sample is sealed, and the **authoritative 6/6** appears. **Do not claim success if Unity has not actually run and passed the required checks.**

# 57. STATE PERSISTENCE AND EVIDENCE HASHING

Implement immutable typed `EvidenceRecord` instances with device ID, sample/fragment ID, acquisition timestamps, calibration version, source graph hash, source measurement data hash, run ID, measured quality, and uncertainty. Historical qualified summaries are separate from the current run. Save/reload during a partially consumed sample, an incomplete CT scan, and a latched W179 fault must preserve consistent state and cannot replenish material or complete objectives without acquiring new evidence. Displayed telemetry values are not direct setters of physical plant state or safety state.

# 58. INSTRUMENT SERVICEABILITY AND REPAIR ECONOMY

Inspect the real Project Spark shop, shelf, purchased equipment and interaction systems before adding service tools. Fictional calibration cartridges, sample vessels and replacement modules should be tracked as inventory where relevant. Repairs require a diagnosed fault, verified safe instrument state, explicit player action, and fresh measurement checks. Consumables have finite quantities and cannot be restored through a task reset. A smoke effect or warning light is not the fault itself. Reject instrument operations and repair claims if the required component, stored sample or valid control connection is unavailable.

# 59. TECHNICAL ART AND SHADER QA — PREVIEW VS PLAYABLE LAB

Validate native Unity materials in the actual project's URP/HDRP/Built-in pipeline, including sRGB albedo/emission vs linear normal/mask data, packed metallic-smoothness channels and glass transparency sorting. Review photon/spectrum hologram readability and avoid excessive glare over precise control text. The source visual rig currently uses a fixed Y axis in `LateUpdate`; honor the authored axes and movement kinds in `Animation_Rigs.csv` instead. Test the level in a normal playable science lab and, if used, the Project Spark central preview table. The table version must contain **visual meshes only**—never duplicate real terminals, physical sample ledger, objectives, player interactions or live physics.

# 60. DELIVERY PHASE GATES AND BLOCKERS

Implement through gates with observable acceptance evidence: **P0** original assets/material scale validation; **P1** live 194-wire graph and real independent terminals; **P2** finite sample custody and inert atmosphere; **P3** CT and spectroscopy data acquisition; **P4** protected vacuum, micro-sample thermal release and mass analysis; **P5** three genuine recoverable faults and PLC; **P6** W179 supervised fault, correction and full fresh qualification; **P7** UI, AAA polish, Editor automation and regressions. At the end of each gate, provide code, a deterministic test, and a status of pass/fail/not-run. If real Project Spark source or Unity Play Mode is unavailable, explicitly report this limitation and deliver untested source without making a fabricated 6/6 claim.

```csv
ObjectiveIndex,Title,AcceptanceCriteria,Implemented
1,Build the Elemental Analysis Network,"194 real user wires, valid SparkTerminal topology, supply returns and independent protection",NO
2,Commission the Pristine Science Laboratory,"Sample custody, inert handling, calibrated CT optical XRF vacuum and mass instruments with fresh evidence",NO
3,Reveal the Hidden Mineral Archive,"Real CT projection coverage, Raman infrared and XRF spectra with valid uncertainty and cross-instrument material map",NO
4,Decode the Ancient Chemical Signature,"Finite mass allocated, protected thermal release, vacuum qualified mass spectra and preserved remainder",NO
5,Recover Three Scientific Instrument Failures,"Actual purge degradation, CT encoder slip, mass detector gain drift, repair and fresh requalification",NO
6,Restore Critical Analytical Chamber Feedback,"Physical W179 disconnect, safe energy inhibit, reconnect, fresh pressure calibration, explicit reset and new full scientific qualification",NO
```

## ANNEX H — AUTHORED PHYSICAL PROCESS CONCEPTS

Source file: `Physical_Analytical_Process_Concept.csv`. Original values follow.

```csv
Process,Plant,Inputs,Outputs,Invariant
Inert archive,Pristine core and sealed glovebox,Finite core sample + inert gas condition,"Custody state, transfer locks, atmosphere contamination witness",No conversion to vacuum without an actual valid transfer
Tomography,Core sample in X-ray shielded stage,"Stage positions, detector projections, calibration",3D reconstruction coverage and spatial uncertainty,Missing angles or encoder drift degrade accuracy
Raman IR XRF,Small selected surface regions,Calibration sources + specimen regions,Raman/IR/XRF spectra with noise/confidence,Elemental peaks alone not a mineral certification
Vacuum analysis,Separate micro sample heated within chamber,"Finite micro sample, power, vacuum pressure","Gas release, ion detector response, thermal state",Do not consume archive main core; finite material conservation
Science fusion,All independent instrument measurements,Timestamped covariance-aware evidence,Qualified cross-instrument interpretation,Display success not evidence
Safety W179,Analytical pressure sensor to independent PLC,Live real feedback not decorative socket continuity,"New fault, reset and postrepair test evidence",Safe shutdown when feedback goes stale
```

## ANNEX K — CRITICAL W179 RECOVERY — ORIGINAL SOURCE TEXT

Source file: `W179_Critical_Analytical_Vacuum_Pressure_Recovery.md`. Original values follow.

```text
# Level 93 — W179 trusted analytical vacuum pressure recovery

- Source: `L93_AnalyticalVacuumPressureTransducer` at decorative anchor `P180` / logical `TRUSTED_CHAMBER_PRESSURE_OUT`.
- Destination: `L93_AnalyticalSafetyPLC` at decorative anchor `P244` / logical `TRUSTED_CHAMBER_PRESSURE_IN`.
- The asset sockets are VISUAL GEOMETRY only. **No live SparkTerminal, wire continuity or PLC is included.**

## Authoritative runtime implementation required
1. Certify objectives 1–5 with real evidence, enter a controlled low-energy instrument qualification phase.
2. Player disconnects the real W179 through the Project Spark wire interaction tool. Circuit graph reports loss of continuity.
3. Invalidate trusted pressure feedback without setting physical pressure to zero; latch missing/stale sensor fault.
4. Inhibit new thermal sample heating and mass analyzer activation; ramp dangerous simulation energy down through physically bounded states.
5. Preserve the pristine core in its separate inert vault. Do not use visual pressure dial as evidence.
6. Player reconnects W179 to compatible terminals; require powered sensor, sample freshness and independent chamber checks.
7. Recalibrate transducer and explicitly reset the safety PLC after actual conditions are qualified.
8. Create a fresh qualification run ID. Collect new CT, optical spectroscopy and mass spectrometry evidence using remaining or reserved sample material.
9. Preserve historical objectives 1–5; award objective 6 only from real `LevelGamePlayManager` evidence and valid post-repair qualification.

No protected instrument simulator, radiation safety, analytical thermal plant or mission script is included in this art package. Implement those in Unity with suitable fictional educational constraints.
```
